| 338 | 🔴→🟢 **First real mailbox: no enquiry could ever be created; the classify dropdown and Open workspace were cut off** | User, 2026-09-17, on joseph@f16sefreight.com (pricing, F16s Base): "not seeing anything, open workspace not working, no dropdown for the operations guy". Found: the Outlook connection and the 1-month import worked (83 messages, 51 conversations) — but (1) **code vs PRD §5.2.3 ("regex stages, the operator mints"):** an enquiry number was only created when someone *changed* the classification to customer enquiry; imported mail arrives already filed as one, so the change was a no-op, Claim created nothing, and with no enquiry the workspace had no work, the Kanban and Enquiries pages were empty, and no shipment could exist to carry the Operator dropdown. 🟢 Claim (and a pricing reply that claims) now creates the enquiry for a conversation filed as a customer enquiry; the workspace shows **Create enquiry** on one that has none; choosing customer enquiry again creates it too. (2) **Classifier — left as it was, by the user's decision:** 39 of Joseph's 51 were colleagues at @f16sefreight.com or automated senders filed as customer enquiries by the fallback. A rule filing own-domain and automated mail as Other was added and then **reverted** the same day at the user's request ("don't change it now … my mails are not what my client usually gets, we'll sort it soon"); Joseph's conversations were restored to their original filing. ❓ Open: revisit the classifier with real client mail. (3) **The classify dropdown was cut off:** the conversation header held Claim, the dropdown, Assign to and Open workspace on one unwrappable line inside a pane that hides overflow, so on a normal screen they ran off the right edge. 🟢 The header wraps; the dropdown is labelled **Filed as**, offers **Other** (the server always accepted it) and shows **Not sorted yet** for unclassified mail. ⚠️ The Operator dropdown still appears only once a shipment (job) exists — Claim → enquiry → Confirm shipment (choose the operator) → job. Tests: RealMailboxFixesTest; full suite 751 passing |
| 339 | 🟢 **Nothing matched → Other, not Customer enquiry; a role changed after sign-in now takes effect** | User, 2026-09-17, on joseph@f16sefreight.com: "all the mails are classified as customer enquiry … when confidence is low just put it in other. Changed to **`other`**, nothing else in the regex: tenant rules → a known client's domain (customer enquiry) → the airline/platform directory → Other. ⚠️ A new prospect not yet added as a client now lands in Other until someone re-files it or adds the client. (2) **The Filed as dropdown was missing** because the browser kept the role from sign-in: Joseph was created as operations (the old Super Admin default, #337), changed to pricing, and still had the operations menu (no Enquiries) and no pricing controls. | 340 | 🟢 **A new company showed 0 credits until the 1st; nothing scheduled ran on the local machine** | User, 2026-09-17: "why does it say 0 credits left?" (joseph@, F16s Base, Tactical). | 342 | 🟢 **A shipment mail the regex had read was filed Other** | User, 2026-09-17: a real mail "Ex BLR RMG 400/18 tons JFK" (Pcs 400, Gross wgt 17400kgs, Ch wt 18000 kgs) came in as Other. Found: the cargo patterns DID read it (gross weight 17,400, high confidence), but filing never looked at what they read — only branch rules, a known client's domain and the airline directory — so with no rules and no clients on F16s Base it fell to the default. Shipments had landed in customer enquiry only because that used to be the default (#339). 🟢 New step before Other: if the patterns read at least one cargo figure with **high** confidence (a labelled weight, pieces, volume), the mail is a **customer enquiry**; a low-confidence read alone (an unlabelled "480 kg", a lane from prose) stays Other. Patterns unchanged. Joseph's unclaimed mail re-filed: only this one changed. ❓ For the regex work later — this mail's figures the patterns MISSED: **pieces** ("Pcs 400"), **chargeable weight** ("Ch wt 18000 kgs"), **dimensions** ("40x30x30 cms"), and the **lane** in the subject ("Ex BLR … JFK"). Test: RealMailboxFixesTest |
| 343 | 🟢 **Customer-enquiry patterns built from the real inbox** | User, 2026-09-17: "why was that mail low confidence? … can you please quote, quote the best rate, give the best rate — all of that matters a lot. Learn the pattern and build proper regex." Trigger: jomygeorge222@gmail.com "blr-ord — Requesting quotation for: BLR-ORD, PCS : 21, WEIGHT : 300 kgs, Dimensions : 60 x 30 x20" was filed Other — `WEIGHT` was not a known label (read as a low-confidence guess), `PCS : 21` was missed (label before the number), dimensions had no pattern, and the words asking for a quote were never looked at. Learnt from all 83 inbound mails in Joseph's inbox. 🟢 (1) **Quote / rate / booking request** (`QUOTE_REQUEST_PATTERN`): RFQ; request(ing) a quote/quotation/rates/booking; please/pls/kindly (send/share/give) your best quote/rates/price; can/could you (share) rates/quote; quote the best/lowest; best/lowest/competitive rate(s)/price; rate request/enquiry; need rates/quote; booking request, confirmed booking. Built NOT to catch our own sales mails ("the commercial quotation for Focus Air", "our best commercial offer") or "Rate your support experience". (2) **Figures:** a plain "Weight / Wgt / Wt" label with a kg unit is a confident gross weight (never a bare table heading); "Ch wt / Chg wt" chargeable; "G.W."; pieces with the label first ("PCS : 21", "Pcs 400", "No. of pieces: 12"); **dimensions** "40x30x30 cms" (new `dimensions` field, shown in the workspace). (3) **Filing:** after rules, known clients and the directory — a quote request OR a figure read with high confidence → customer enquiry; otherwise Other. Result on the real inbox: exactly the 3 shipment mails are enquiries, the other 80 are not. Staged figures refreshed on Joseph's enquiry conversations. ⚠️ Still not read: a lane with no separator ("BLR JFK", "Ex BLR … JFK") and weights in tons ("18 tons"). ❓ #7101 ("Re: 125-23736134", an AWB fix) is still filed as a customer enquiry — it was set before and is left as it is. Tests: EnquiryPatternsTest (the user's phrases, both real shipment mails, six real non-enquiries) |
| 344 | 🟢 **Mail shown as it was written; blank lines kept when sending; Extract on every enquiry PDF** | User, 2026-09-17 (screenshot of "RE: 125-23736134"). (1) **Why that conversation is a customer enquiry:** not the classifier — `email_classification_overrides` shows Joseph George changed it Other → customer enquiry at 19:24 with Filed as, which created ENQA-BASEBAS-26-0001. Left as it is. (2) **Formatting — code vs request:** the conversation showed only `body_snippet`, a 500-character plain-text preview; the real mail was never read. 🟢 `GET /api/inbox/messages/{message}/body` fetches the full HTML from Outlook the first time a message is opened (`GraphMailProvider::body`), cleans it with a display allow-list that keeps spacing, bold/italic/underline, colours, fonts and sizes, alignment, tables and images but removes scripts, forms, frames, event handlers and javascript: links (`MailBody::forDisplay`), stores it in `email_messages.body_storage_path` (deleted with the message by `mail:prune`) and shows it in a sandboxed frame (`MailBodyFrame.vue`, no scripts) as tall as the mail. Falls back to the preview when the mailbox cannot give it. Verified on Joseph's real mails. **Sending:** a blank line typed in the editor was an empty paragraph with no height, so the gap vanished — now kept. ⚠️ The composer still has only its eight controls (bold, italic, underline, lists, link, quote): no text colour or size; say if those should be added. (3) **Extract:** was shown only after the shipment was confirmed (a job existed). It is now on every PDF of a customer-enquiry conversation for pricing and operations; before a shipment exists it opens the workspace at "Did this shipment confirm?" with the file named, and the file goes into Extraction on confirm. Tests: MailFormattingTest |

| 346 | 🟢 **A mail shows only itself, at the app's size** | User, 2026-09-17 (screenshot of "RE: blr-ord"): the reply showed the client's whole earlier mail under it, "already there in the loop", and the font was too big. 🟢 `MailBody::withoutQuotedMail` cuts the body at the first marker a mail client writes above its quote — Outlook web / new Outlook (`appendonsend`, `divRplyFwdMsg`), Outlook desktop (the grey rule above "From:"), Gmail (`gmail_quote`), Apple Mail (`blockquote type=cite`), Yahoo — unless nothing would be left (a bare forward keeps it). The four bodies already stored were cleared and are fetched again. Test: MailFormattingTest |
| 347 | 🔴→🟢 **Doc vs request: an enquiry number as soon as mail is filed a customer enquiry; re-filing removes it** | User, 2026-09-17: the Kanban showed 1 of the 4 customer enquiries. Don't mark as lost because it could just not be an enquiry"; claimed enquiries still quoting stay **off** the Kanban (they are on the Enquiries page). **Doc changes:** PRD §5.2.3/§5.2.5 said "regex stages, the operator mints — no enquiry_no is consumed until an operator confirms"; PRD §1 said a demoted enquiry is marked lost, never deleted. 🟢 `App\Services\EnquiryMinter` (one place): mail the classifier files as a customer enquiry gets its enquiry as it arrives (`MessageIngestor`); Filed as → customer enquiry creates it; Filed as → anything else **deletes** the enquiry (its passes with it; AI/credit logs unlinked) and its number is not reused; still refused once a shipment exists. | 348 | 🟢 **Confirming a shipment adds its client; the whole mail flow tested end to end** | User, 2026-09-17: "when the shipment is confirmed you'll add the domain name there right? Test that all these things are working." Code vs expectation: nothing created a client — Clients & Partners filled only by hand (#315 left this open). 🟢 `ClientContacts::clientForConfirmedEnquiry`, run inside `POST /api/enquiries/{enquiry}/convert` when the enquiry has no client: from the conversation's first sender — a company domain finds the client with that domain or adds one named by the domain (mail already received from it is gathered into its contacts); a free-mail sender (gmail.com…) finds or adds a client named by the **address**, with no domain; a sender on our own company's domain (a colleague forwarding the request) adds **no** client. The sender is saved as a contact; the enquiry and the job carry the client. ⚠️ A client added this way is named by its domain or address — write the real company name in on the Clients page. **End-to-end test** `RealMailFlowTest`, through the API with Outlook faked: a quote request arrives → customer enquiry with its number → in the Kanban pool → the mail shows only itself → Claim sends the acknowledgement as a reply and makes the claimer owner of the conversation and the enquiry → it leaves the pool and is on Enquiries → Confirm shipment with an operator → job in Processing with that operator on the workspace → client added with the sender as a contact → a New mail goes out keeping its blank line; plus Gmail and colleague senders on confirm, and re-filing removing the enquiry. Full suite 763, JS 96 passing |
| 349 | 🟢 **Super Admin → Mail filing: how the regex files mail and how often people change it** | User, 2026-09-17: "are we measuring how many times they change it from customer enquiry to anything else? … show in superadmin so we can work on the regex." Found: every Filed as change was recorded (`email_classification_overrides`: from, to, who, sender, subject, snippet; kept after the 3-month mail clean-up), but nothing showed it — the grouped summary endpoint had no screen, the Monitor's "Export classification overrides" was a plain link without the sign-in so it was refused, and with no record of what the regex itself filed there was no rate. 🟢 New `email_threads.auto_classification` (migration 2026_09_17_100000; set by `MessageIngestor` when the regex files a mail; existing conversations backfilled from the first change, else their current filing). New page **Super Admin → Mail filing** (`GET /api/admin/mail-filing`, company + 7/30/90 days): for each type the regex filed — mails, changed by a person, changed %, changed to what; **missed enquiries** (changed to customer enquiry); the sender domains changed most often with an example subject; the 50 latest changes with company, sender, subject, the words the regex read and who changed it; **Export changes (CSV)** for that company, downloaded through the signed-in request. The Monitor's broken export link now opens this page. F16s Base today: 49 filed Other (1 changed → customer enquiry, 2%), 3 filed customer enquiry (none changed). Test: MailFilingReportTest |
| 350 | 🔴→🟢 **"Cancel the job first" — there was no way to cancel, and cancelling would not have helped** | User, 2026-09-17: "how to cancel: This conversation already has a shipment against it. Cancel the job first." Joseph had confirmed JOBA-BASEBAS-26-0001 on "RE: 125-23736134". Found: (1) **code vs PRD §5.4 State 4** — `POST /api/jobs/{job}/cancel` existed, but the **[Cancel Shipment]** button with its fixed reasons was never built, so nobody could cancel; the endpoint had no permission check and accepted any reason text. (2) Re-filing counted cancelled shipments too, so even after cancelling it would still refuse, and the enquiry could not be removed (the shipment row must be kept). 🟢 **Cancel shipment** in the workspace header for pricing (the same permission as confirming), a pop-up with the PRD's eleven reasons (Other needs words), saying the shipment is kept for review and its AWB returns to stock; the server now checks the permission, accepts only those reasons and refuses a second cancel. Re-filing is blocked only by a shipment still in progress — its message now says where to cancel; with only a cancelled shipment, the conversation lets go of the enquiry, and the enquiry and cancelled shipment are kept. Test: RealMailFlowTest |
| 351 | 🟢 **A picture under the signature; Cancel shipment beside Filed as** | User, 2026-09-17: "add a way to add signature image" and "where to cancel?". (1) Signatures were text and the eight editor styles only — a pasted logo was stripped. 🟢 Settings → Mailboxes → each mailbox's signature: **Add image / Replace image / Remove image** (PNG, JPG or GIF up to 500 KB, virus-scanned; migration 2026_09_17_110000 `mailbox_connections.signature_image_path/_mime`; `POST|DELETE /api/user/mailboxes/{mailbox}/signature-image`). It is sent **inside the mail** as an inline attachment referenced `cid:` — as Outlook sends a logo — because a link to our server shows nothing until the app has a public address and data: pictures are blocked by Gmail and Outlook. (2) Cancel shipment (#350) sat only in the workspace header; it is now also beside Filed as at the top of the conversation. Test: SignatureImageTest |

| 353 | 🟢 **Reading a document survives changing tab, closing the workspace and a reload** | User, 2026-09-18: "when I click Choose files and extract it starts, but when I move the tab why does it stop? It should run in the background." Found: the server already reads documents in the background (`ProcessPdfOcrJob` on the `pdf_processing` queue) — it was the SCREEN that gave up. The Extraction panel sat in a `v-if` chain with Cost sheet and Credits, so choosing another tab destroyed it: its timers were cleared in `beforeDestroy`, the staged documents and the reading were gone, and the finished extraction was never collected. 🟢 The panel now stays mounted while the workspace is open (`v-show`), and on opening it asks `GET /api/user/ocr-running` — documents still being read, plus any finished in the last 10 minutes, for that person only, from the last 2 hours — and puts them back in the list, still reading, with their timer. So a tab change, a closed workspace, another conversation or a page reload all pick the reading up again. ⚠️ `QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` locally, so the reading actually runs inside the upload request: it finishes even if the screen moves on, but closing the browser mid-request kills it. For true background reading set `QUEUE_CONNECTION=redis` in `.env` and run `php artisan queue:work --queue=pdf_processing`. Test: ExtractionResumesTest |

(2) **Settings → Finance** (`/settings/finance`, accounts and the Boss at Command, reached from Financials rather than the rail — it is set up once, and a seventh rail item would compete with the registers worked daily): the **chart of accounts** per branch with how many postings each has, named and renamed here (a code is never changed — the ledger points at it — and accounts appear by themselves the first time something posts), and the **rate cards** — a rate agreed with one client or supplier for a charge, a weight break and a date range, added and removed. ⚠️ `rate_cards` requires the end of both ranges, so the form asks for them: an open-ended rate is how last year's price is still quoted next year. ❓ No bank-account master exists (`bank_transactions` carries only the feed's own id), so "banks" from PRD §2.4 is not built. | 369 | 🟢 **Credited vs billed, the mail that asks about it, and an inbox for accounts** | User, 2026-09-19: "the magic is we connect Setu or Yoodlee … we can check what actually got credited and what was mentioned in the bill, and our AI can draft a mail to the company (set up inbox for accounts as well)". PRD §6.4 names **Setu (India)** and Plaid elsewhere, read-only. (1) **A bank row now says what the bank said**: provider, value date, credit or debit, narration, counterparty, reference and currency (migration 2026_09_19_090000) — an amount alone cannot be compared with a bill. `plaid_transaction_id` stays the provider's own id and its UNIQUE index stays the ingestion guard, whichever provider. (2) **One road in** (`Bank\StatementImporter`, `POST /api/reconciliation/import`): a bank's CSV today — the column spellings Indian banks use — the Setu or Plaid feed on the same road later; sending the same statement twice refreshes rather than double-counts, and a line with no reference is skipped rather than guessed. (3) **`GET /api/reconciliation/differences`**: paid short, paid more than billed, and money that fits no invoice, each with billed, credited, the difference and what the bank narration says. (4) **`POST /api/reconciliation/{transaction}/draft-query`**: Gemma writes the query mail from those facts with the same any-new-number-is-thrown-out rule and a plain template otherwise, addressed to the client's own contacts; accounts edit it and send it from their own mailbox through the ordinary compose path — nothing sends itself. (5) **Accounts have an inbox**: `viewInbox` includes accounts, they see only the mail they are on (like sales and the Boss), they cannot claim a shipment, and their rail is Financials · Inbox · Clients & Partners · Settings. Test: BankDifferencesTest. ❓ Next: CASS generalised to vendor statements per supplier type (airline, trucking, misc) |
| 370 | 🟢 **CASS generalised: a statement from any supplier, against our own vouchers** | User, 2026-09-19: "about the CASS, create based on per client — vendor could be airlines, trucking, misc; add all types of options here". An airline's CASS, a trucker's month, a broker's or a warehouse's all ask one question: does what they billed agree with what we booked? So there is no CASS feature — there is a **supplier statement**, and the supplier type only narrows the picker (all nine `Partner::TYPES`). (1) **Their line is matched to a SHIPMENT, not to a voucher.** An airline bills an AWB, a trucker bills a docket against a job; the shipment is the only thing both sides name, so `VendorStatements::shipmentFor()` normalises an AWB in any shape (`17610000001`, `176-10000008`, `176 10000008`) and otherwise reads our job number. ⚠️ Nothing is ever matched by amount: two shipments to the same airline in a month routinely cost the same, and a price match would attribute a cost to the wrong job. (2) **Our figure is that supplier's vouchers only.** `ourCost()` sums `accounts_purchase_items.net_amount` through vouchers where `vendor_id` is theirs — a trucker's voucher must never answer for the airline's line; tested (a job with the trucker's cost booked reads `not_booked` on the airline's statement). Each line lands in one of four states: **agreed** (within ₹1 — rounding is not a dispute), **different**, **not_booked** (the shipment is ours, the cost is not), **unmatched** (no such shipment). (3) **A period sent again replaces it** — a statement is their whole word for that month, and keeping both copies doubles what they appear to be owed (unique `[agent_id, vendor_id, period]`). **Compare again** re-runs the comparison, because vouchers move: a line reading "we have no cost booked" last week is agreed today. (4) **The query mail** goes to the supplier's own address with every disagreeing line spelled out — what they billed, what we booked, the difference — through the same drafter as the client payment queries, so the same rule holds: any number Gemma writes that is not in the facts throws the answer away and the plain template is used. Nothing sends; accounts edit it and send it from their own mailbox. A statement where everything agrees refuses to draft (`nothing_to_query`) rather than write an empty mail. (5) A line queried with them carries a **dispute note** in words, and the statement reads `disputed`. New: `vendor_statements` + `vendor_statement_lines`, `app/Services/Bank/VendorStatements.php`, `VendorStatementController`, a `vendor_difference` goal on `PaymentQueryDrafter`, and the **Supplier statements** view on Financials. 🔴 Fixed in passing: `branchForImport` was a *method* read as a value in the bank import (so the guard never fired and the branch went across as a function) — it is a computed property now. Tests: `VendorStatementsTest` (5). ❓ Next from the finance map: ageing & collections, receipts against documents, and the journal with drill-through. |
| 371 | 🟢 **The Billing section, built against Logi-Sys** | User, 2026-09-19, with a screenshot of `ksrfreight.mylogisys.com` Billing: "look at whatever is in the billing section and build it so that it matches … put demo data to test all this … almost the same columns and remove the columns that might not be needed." Their menu is Forwarding (Invoice, Revenue Debit Note, Revenue Credit Note, Brokerage Invoice, Consol Invoice), Receipts, Data Export, Multiple Bill Printing, E-Invoice. **Those five documents were already in PRD §6.2 and already shared `accounts_invoices`; none of them could be raised.** Now: (1) **One register over all five types** at `/billing`, with the columns their Multiple Bill Printing prints — Trans No., Date, Type, Organization, Curr, Amount, **Amount (INR)** — plus Shipment, Outstanding and Status, which their screen lacks and our desk needs. **Dropped as not ours:** *Sign Status* and *Method* (digital signing — we do not sign documents), *Approved By* (we have no approval step; finalize/post is the Status column), and *Print Copy For* (one copy, to the organization). *LOB* is our transport mode and is on the shipment. *Exclude Reverse Txns* is kept, in words: **Exclude credit notes**. (2) 🔴 **A credit note counts the other way in a total.** Its face reads positive — that is what the client receives — but in the register it gives money back, so the rows print what the document says and the totals net (`direction`), with the credited figure said under the table. The same rule on the printed bill: a credit note says "credit to be adjusted against your account", never "Outstanding". (3) **Each type numbers off its own counter** — INV / DN / CN / BRK / CSINV (PRD §6.3); `finalize` used a hard-coded `'INV'` for every type. (4) 🔴 **A credit note may not give back more than the invoice was worth**, checked at finalize against the other notes already raised (PRD §6.2), and the room is shown before it is typed. (5) 🔴 **Credit notes post as the MIRROR, not as negative revenue**: Dr `4900-Sales-Adjustments`, Dr `2200-GST-Output`, Cr `1200-AR`. Brokerage posts to `1210-Commission-Receivable` / `4800-Commission-Revenue`, consol to `1220-AR-Agents` / `4050-Consol-Revenue` — an agent is not a client and commission is not freight, so neither touches `1200-AR` or the customer ageing. (6) **Receipts** are their own document: `accounts_receipts` + `accounts_receipt_allocations`, because one payment settles several invoices and one invoice takes several payments. `amount_paid` is re-derived from the allocations, never incremented. Over-allocation is refused, not clamped. (7) **Multiple Bill Printing** is the register with checkboxes: Print (one PDF, page-broken), Send mail (🔴 one mail per document — two clients' bills in one envelope is a disclosure), Data export (CSV of the same query, so screen and file cannot disagree). (8) ❓ **E-Invoice records, it does not mint.** An IRN comes from the IRP through a GSP on the company's own credentials, which we do not hold; the register shows what is registered, what is waiting, and what never needed it (no client GSTIN = B2C), and says so on screen. New: `BillingController`, `ReceiptController`, `Support/BillingDocuments`, `Services/Billing/BillPdf`, `documents/bill.blade.php`, `Billing.vue` (accounts' rail; the Boss reads it from Financials), `BillingDemoSeeder` (**DEMO tenant only** — all five types in two branches and two currencies, receipts settled in full, in part and on account, one invoice through the IRP). 🔴 Fixed in passing: `ApiService.get(resource, slug)` cannot carry an axios config, so a blob POST needed `postForFile`. Tests: `BillingDeskTest` (10). ❓ Next: ageing & collections, and the journal with drill-through. |
| 372 | 🟢 **The billing documents are workable, not just listed** | User, 2026-09-19 on #371: "you just created the billing page exactly like that — what about the credit note and everything. work on the entire billing page." Correct: the register listed five document types and **no document could be opened**. A row went nowhere, a draft's lines could not be touched, and finalize, post, void and print all lived on other screens or nowhere. Now: (1) **One section per document**, as Logi-Sys lays their Forwarding submenu out — All documents, Invoices, Debit notes, Credit notes, Brokerage, Consol — plus Receipts and E-Invoice, each with a **New …** that opens the right form pre-set to that type. (2) **A row opens the document in a drawer**, in ONE round trip (`GET /billing/{id}`): header, lines, who it is addressed to, what it amends, the notes raised against it, the money received against it, and the journal posting would write. ⚠️ A drawer that fetched lines, then receipts, then the journal would show a document half one version and half another while it loaded. (3) **A draft is edited line by line** — add, change, remove, and a header (dates, currency, rate, narration) — and 🔴 **the header always follows the lines**: subtotal, tax and grand total are re-added from the items on every change, never typed. A finalized document refuses the edit and says to raise a note instead. (4) **Finalize and Post are on the document**, where the person deciding is looking at it. (5) **Void is a status with a reason against it** (PRD §9.3) — never a delete, so the number never leaves the register — and is refused once the document is posted (raise a credit note) or once money has arrived against it (reverse the receipt). 🔴 The `can.void` flag carries **both** rules, so the button is never offered only to refuse: it was, on a paid invoice, until this was caught by opening a real one. (6) **An ordinary invoice can now be raised from Billing** (`type = invoice`, job + client), not only from pricing's cost sheet — their Billing → Invoice screen does. (7) A note raised from the drawer opens pre-filled against the document it was raised from, and a document raised anywhere opens straight into the drawer — a draft nobody is looking at is a draft nobody finalizes. New: `show`, `update`, `storeLine`, `updateLine`, `destroyLine`, `void` on `BillingController`; `Billing.vue` rebuilt around `FxDrawer`. 🔴 Vue 2: a `#slot` template must be a direct child of the receiving component — nesting `#meta` inside `<template v-if>` fails the template compile outright. Tests: `BillingDeskTest` now 13. ❓ Next: ageing & collections, and the journal with drill-through. |
| 373 | 🟢 **Ageing and collections** | User, 2026-09-20: "now build the ageing and collections page." Two halves of one job on one screen — **who owes what and for how long**, and **what has been done about it** — because an ageing nobody acts on is a report, and a chase queue without the ageing beside it is a to-do list nobody trusts. (1) **Buckets: not due / 1–30 / 31–60 / 61–90 / 90+,** measured 🔴 **from the DUE date, not the document date** — a bill raised on the 1st with 30 days to pay is not overdue on the 2nd. Where no due date was ever set the document date stands in and the screen says **(assumed)** rather than being silently generous. Every figure is INR at each document's own rate, so a column can be totalled. (2) 🔴 **A credit note SUBTRACTS.** Its face is positive — that is what the client receives — but it reduces what they owe. **And `CreditGateService` had exactly this bug**: a finalized credit note with a `customer_id` was summed as exposure, so giving a client ₹15,000 back *tightened* their credit gate by ₹15,000. Both now read the same definition (`AgeingService::OWED`), because two definitions of "what they owe" is two answers to the one question the desk asks. (3) 🔴 **Grouping by `billed_party_id` alone split one client into two rows** — a NULL row of 24 documents and a real row of 1, reading as two organizations owing half each. Invoices raised before that column was filled in carry only `customer_id`; the query now coalesces, and the demo tenant went from 6 "organizations" to the 5 that exist. Found by opening the real ageing, not by a test. (4) **Agents are aged beside clients.** A brokerage or consol bill is a receivable like any other, so a partner-billed document appears in the ageing under the partner — but never in the customer credit gate, which is a client-only concept. (5) **The queue is ordered by what is DUE TO BE ACTIONED, not by what is largest**: broken promises first, then whoever nobody has called at all, then by size. The biggest debtor is often the one already promising to pay on Friday; the one nobody has called is the one that becomes a write-off. (6) 🔴 **A promise is a DATE and an AMOUNT, not a note** — "they said next week" cannot be sorted, reported on, or shown to have been broken. `collection_follow_ups` holds what was said, what was promised by when, and when to ask again; closing one is **kept / broken / closed**, never a delete, because a broken promise is the most useful row in the table the next time that client asks for terms. (7) **The chasing mail** is drafted from what is actually overdue (a bill not yet due is never chased), goes to the client's own contacts, sends from the person's own mailbox and logs itself against them — a chase nobody recorded did not happen. Same no-invented-numbers rule as every other drafted mail. New: `AgeingService`, `CollectionsController`, `collection_follow_ups`, a `collection` goal on `PaymentQueryDrafter`, `Collections.vue` (accounts' rail; the Boss reads it from Financials), and demo chases in `BillingDemoSeeder` so the queue shows all three states at once. Tests: `AgeingAndCollectionsTest` (10). ❓ Next: the journal with drill-through, and job/client/lane profitability. |
| 374 | 🟢 **The day book, and drill-through from a report to the document** | User, 2026-09-20, after asking what it meant: "yes build it." The ledger was being written correctly and could only be read one document at a time — there was no register across documents, and no way to open a figure on the P&L. (1) **The day book**: every posting, newest first, filtered by account, branch, period, date range, document type and side, with the document number and the organization on every line, and a CSV export. Debits and credits are totalled and the screen says plainly when they do not agree. ⚠️ The document is put on each row by three small lookups keyed on `source_type`, not by three LEFT JOINs — joined, the plan scanned all three document tables for every row, and this page has to open on a ledger of a hundred thousand entries. (2) **An account ledger** when one account is picked: 🔴 **opening balance from every entry BEFORE the window**, the movements, a running balance down the page, and the closing figure — which is the number the trial balance carries. An account ledger that starts at zero on the 1st is not a ledger, it is a filter, and its closing figure will not agree with the one report it must agree with. 🔴 `reorder()`, not another `orderBy`: Laravel APPENDS an order, so the day book's newest-first ordering still won and the running balance read up the page. (3) **Drill-through**, four levels: a P&L / balance-sheet / trial-balance line → that account's ledger → a posting → ⚠️ **the WHOLE journal entry, not the line clicked** (the question is always "against what?") → the document itself with its own lines, and for a sales document a link that opens it in Billing. Every account in the drilled entry is itself a link into its ledger. (4) 🔴 **A bank match was posting against the wrong document.** `ReconciliationController` wrote `source_type = 'receipt'` with the INVOICE's id as `source_id`, so drilling through would land on the invoice as if it were a receipt — and the money never appeared in the receipts register at all. A match now raises a real `accounts_receipts` row with its allocation (using the `bank_transaction_id` column that existed for exactly this), posts against it, and `unmatch` asks the receipt whether it is posted instead of guessing from a ledger row keyed on an invoice. (5) `BillingDemoSeeder` now posts ~24 documents per run through the same `LedgerPostingService` the controllers use — a seeder writing its own journal lines would put figures in the ledger the product would never produce, and the trial balance would prove nothing. The demo tenant reads 62 postings, balanced at ₹2,114,347.76. New: `JournalController` (GET only — there is no write endpoint on the ledger, and a posting is corrected by another posting), `Journal.vue`, report lines linked. Tests: `JournalDrillThroughTest` (7). ❓ Next: job/client/lane profitability. |
| 375 | 🟢 **Job, client and lane profitability** | User, 2026-09-20: "now build the job client and lane profitability." Three views of ONE calculation — a client roll-up that disagreed with the shipments under it would leave the desk choosing which screen to believe, so both roll-ups are the shipment list grouped, and a test asserts the totals of all three agree. (1) 🔴 **PROFIT IS NET OF TAX on both sides.** Revenue is the invoice **subtotal**, cost is the voucher line **amount** — never the grand totals. GST is a liability we collect and an asset we reclaim; folding either in overstates every shipment by the tax on it, and a lane that looks 18% better than it is gets priced accordingly. (2) 🔴 **A credit note subtracts revenue, a debit note adds it** — the same signing rule as the ageing, the credit gate and the billing register. Drafts and voids are not revenue: one was never sent, the other never stood. Foreign-currency revenue counts at its own document's rate. (3) ⚠️ **A job's whole P&L stays together.** The window filters on the SHIPMENT's own date (`completed_at`, else `created_at`) and then every rupee billed and booked against it counts, whenever the paperwork was raised — filtering on document date would split one shipment across two months and report both halves as thin. (4) 🔴 **The two lies a profitability report tells, named on the row and counted above the table**: `no_cost_booked` (billed, nothing costed — reads as a 100% margin that is not real) and `not_billed` (costed, nothing billed yet). `margin_pct` is **NULL, never 0%**, on no revenue. (5) ⚠️ **Losses sort first by default.** Sorted by revenue you get the list you already know; the row worth opening is the one that lost money. (6) **Lanes come from the enquiry's `origin_code` → `dest_code`**, named from `locations` for air. ❓ **Sea LOCODEs do not resolve: `ports` is empty (0 rows)** while sea enquiries already carry INMAA/SGSIN-style codes — those lanes read as bare codes until it is populated. (7) `BillingDemoSeeder` now books the buy side (30 vouchers: carrier, trucking, clearance), because without it every shipment reads 100% and the report is a revenue report with a warning on every row; one shipment in five is deliberately sold below cost, since a report where nothing ever loses money is one nobody opens twice. Demo reads 10 shipments, ₹1,32,87,130 revenue against ₹26,37,916 cost, one loss-making shipment at −9%. New: `ProfitabilityService`, `ProfitabilityController`, `Profitability.vue`. Tests: `ProfitabilityTest` (9). ❓ Next from the finance map: GST returns (GSTR-1/3B), TDS, payment runs, bank accounts master. |
| 376 | 🟢 **The port directory is loaded: sea lanes read as ports, not as five-letter codes** | User, 2026-09-20: "yes load the port codes", on the gap flagged in #375 — `ports` was created empty in August and stayed empty, so every sea lane on the profitability report printed `INMAA → SGSIN`. 🔴 **`locations` names AIRPORTS by IATA and nothing else; a three-letter code cannot address DEHAM**, which is why the two tables exist. `python/export_ports.py` pulls the UN/LOCODE list (the Frictionless mirror of the UNECE publication, 7.3 MB, 116,213 entries) and `PortSeeder` loads it; `storage/app/ports.json` is gitignored like `locations.json`, and the exporter is the reproducible step. (1) **Only freight NODES are kept**, read off the 8-character Function mask: position 1 `1` seaport → **sea** (17,520), position 4 `4` airport → **air** (7,246), position 8 `B` border crossing → **land** (392). The other **90,473 road points are villages** — loading them would drown every picker in places no forwarder has ever quoted. A location that is both seaport and airport is one row (the table is keyed on the LOCODE) filed as `sea`, because the air side already resolves through `locations`. **25,158 loaded.** (2) ⚠️ **Status `XX` ("will be removed") is skipped; everything else loads ACTIVE, `QQ` included.** `QQ` means the NAME is under query, not that the port is shut — **Jebel Ali carries it**, and it is on live enquiries. Inferring `is_active` from a naming query would drop one of the busiest ports in the world out of the pickers. (3) `NameWoDiacritics`, not `Name`: the diacritic-free form UN/LOCODE itself uses in EDI, and these names go onto customs paperwork. (4) 🔴 **DELETE, not TRUNCATE.** MySQL refuses to truncate a table that is the target of a foreign key **at all** — `rate_cards.destination_port_id` alone is enough, with nothing pointing at it (SQLSTATE 42000 / 1701). A delete clears the rows and still fails, correctly, on any row something really references; the seeder also refuses in words when rate cards, users or customers already point here. (5) `ProfitabilityService::placeNames()` now reads **both** directories — three characters is IATA, five is a LOCODE, so they cannot collide. The demo bills one sea shipment per branch so the lane report exercises it: **INMAA → SGSIN now reads "Chennai (ex Madras) → Singapore"**. ⚠️ `python/export_ports.py` avoids `str | None` annotations: this machine runs Python 3.9, where that syntax is a TypeError at import rather than a warning. Tests: `ProfitabilityTest` now 10. |
| 377 | 🟢 **The client book: every branch's clients, with a rep and a credit limit on each** | User, 2026-09-20: "we have no place to show all the clients of all the branches, a full onboarding is done — check the PRD for the columns — and then a sales person is to be attached to it with a credit limit to it." The **columns all existed** (`customers` has carried gst/pan/duns, bank, terms, credit_limit, default_port_id, branch_id, sales_id since Batch 1a); what was missing was the screen, and half of them could not be SET — `validated()` accepted only name, domain, email, phone, address and sales_id, so branch, port, DUNS and bank details were unreachable through the API. (1) **`Clients.vue`**: every branch in one list — Client · Branch · Salesperson · GSTIN · Terms · Credit limit · Owed now · Left · Contacts — filterable by branch, by rep, by **no salesperson** and by **no credit limit**, and groupable by domain, because **the group IS `(company_id, email_domain)`** (PRD §2.2): one client with five GSTINs is five rows sharing a domain, and Indian GST makes that the normal case, not an edge one. (2) **Onboarding** captures every PRD column in four sections — who they are, who owns them, tax and legal, terms and credit, their bank. (3) 🔴 **`sales_id` and `branch_id` are now checked against the acting tenant.** `exists:users,id` passes for **any user on the platform**, and `sales_id` is the scoping key for the entire Command-tier client book — an id typed or guessed would have moved the client into a stranger's rep's book and out of ours. Both are refused with 422; tested against a second company's rep and branch. (4) 🔴 **A NULL credit limit is "not configured" and never blocks; `0.00` blocks every shipment.** The book prints **Not set**, never `0` — and the test pins both, because reading one as the other is the difference between cargo moving and cargo stopping. (5) ⚠️ **"Owed now" nets credit notes**, the same rule as the ageing, the credit gate and the billing register, in **one grouped query** — a credit check per row turns a 50-row directory into 150 queries on the page the Boss leaves open. (6) A client with **nobody** on it prints `Nobody` in red rather than a blank: it is in no rep's book, and a blank cell reads as "not loaded". (7) Bank details are written, encrypted at rest and **never returned** — the form says so and asks for re-entry to change them. (8) The port picker **searches** rather than lists: 25,158 rows is not a dropdown. ❓ **Proximity routing is not built** — PRD §2.2 resolves the branch by LOCODE proximity and auto-assigns a rep from it, but `ports` carries no coordinates, so both are explicit fields defaulting to the creating user's branch. Guessing a branch wrongly is worse than asking. Plan: **implementation_guide §11** now carries the whole accounts re-layout, with the client book as §11.4 and the migration order in §11.5. | 378 | 🟢 **Today — the accounts desk lands on work, not on a register** (guide §11.5 step 2) | User, 2026-09-20: "do it", on the re-layout plan. Accounts used to land on `Financials → Waiting to be billed` and had to already know that was where the day started; nothing anywhere counted what was waiting. `/today` is now their home (`HOME.accounts`, first rail item). (1) 🔴 **Every card is a COUNT AND A FIGURE, and every card is a link.** "6 payments" could be ₹6,000 or ₹6 lakh, and ₹6 lakh could be one payment or sixty — either number alone leaves you guessing, and a card you cannot click is a fact nobody can act on. Four cards in the order money moves: **To bill** (cost sheets handed over and unbilled) · **Money to place** (credited, not against an invoice) · **Overdue** · **Not posted**. (2) ⚠️ **No card computes its own version of anything.** Each runs the query its own screen runs — the overdue card goes through `AgeingService` — so the home can never disagree with the register it links to, which is the fastest way to lose the desk's trust in both. A test pins the overdue figure against `/collections`'s own summary. (3) **Six exceptions, each of which clears itself** when the thing is dealt with, because a list carrying standing conditions stops being read inside a week: **no open period for today** (critical — nothing can post, and that is invisible until a post is refused) · **period ends within 7 days** · **clients over their credit limit**, named, before the gate stops the cargo · **supplier statement lines that disagree** · 🔴 **billed shipments with no cost booked**, whose margin reads far too high · **cost sheets waiting over a week** (PRD §6.2's seven-day line). (4) A clear desk **says so** — "Nothing is waiting on you. The period is open, nobody is over their limit, and every supplier statement agrees" — rather than showing four zeroes. Tests: `AccountsTodayTest` (9). ⚠️ **Two Claude sessions were working this repo and this database at once** (2026-09-20). A full-suite run during the other session's own test run reports 270 scattered failures — inflated counts, unique-key collisions — because both write `f16s_test`; targeted runs between theirs are clean (57 across the six finance suites). Commits from here are staged by explicit path, never `git add -A`: `cfb4e93e` had already swept ~12 of the other session's files in under a ports commit message. ❓ Next per guide §11.5: **Money in** — Billing + Collections + bank matching as one five-stage pipeline. |
| 379 | 🔴→🟢 **The unposted queue was never written to, so a month could always be closed** | Found while building the regression fixture (user, 2026-09-20: "be extremely careful as this is all money related"). `unposted_transactions_queue` has existed since Batch 1a. `LedgerPostingService::write()` deleted from it, `RegisterController` read it, the Today card counted it — and **no code path ever inserted a row**. Two consequences: "Not yet posted" was permanently empty, and 🔴 **`closePeriod()` counts that queue to decide whether a month may be signed off, so it would always allow the close** however many documents were still outside the ledger. That is the guard that stops a period being closed with revenue missing from it. 🟢 `UnpostedQueue` + `UnpostedDocumentObserver` now track every `AccountsInvoice` and `AccountsPurchaseVoucher` from creation: the row carries the document's live total, follows it as the total changes, and leaves the queue when the document posts (the delete already existed) **or is voided** — a void document will never reach the ledger, so it is not waiting for anything. ⚠️ An observer rather than a call at each creation site: invoices are raised from the cost sheet, from the billing desk and from seeders, and a queue depending on all three remembering is wrong the first time a fourth path appears. ⚠️ Bookkeeping never breaks the document — a queue row that cannot be attributed to a user is skipped rather than throwing, because a missing worklist row is a gap and a refused invoice is a shipment that cannot be billed. |
| 380 | 🟢 **A money fixture whose every figure is known, and a command that checks all 81 of them** | User, 2026-09-20: "build a regress demo seeder and check all the numbers, be extremely careful as this is all money related … only command view has access to it." `AccountsRegressionSeeder` + `php artisan accounts:verify`. (1) 🔴 **The numbers are the point.** Every amount in the fixture is a round figure chosen so each derived total can be worked out **on paper**; the expected answers are written by hand in the verifier and 🔴 **not derived from the code under test** — a verifier that recomputed them with the same services would agree with any bug in them. 81 assertions: profitability by shipment/client/lane, the five ageing buckets, credit exposure and holds, all four Today cards and its exceptions, eleven ledger account balances, the trial balance, the P&L, the balance sheet, and the day book. (2) 🔴 **Two tenants with deliberately different arithmetic** — Regression Freight in multiples of 1,000, Rival Freight in multiples of 7,777 — so a cross-tenant leak shows up as a **wrong number**, not as a screen that merely looks busier. Isolation verified: each accounts login sees one branch (its own), its own clients, its own revenue, its own journal. (3) ⚠️ **The most valuable assertions are the cross-checks**, where two figures built by different code from different tables must agree: **P&L net == profitability margin** (₹1,70,000), P&L revenue == profitability revenue, P&L expense == profitability cost, assets − liabilities == net profit, and **AR ledger balance + money on account == the ageing total** (₹4,56,800 + ₹50,000 = ₹5,06,800). A single figure can be wrong and plausible; two derived along different paths cannot be wrong in the same direction by accident. (4) 🔒 A Tactical tenant's accounts login is **refused at the gate** on all eight endpoints, not shown an empty register — "we have no invoices" is a different and much worse answer than "your plan does not include this". (5) The fixture carries its own places (six IATA codes, two LOCODEs) so it does not fail on a clean database for a reason unrelated to money, and `accounts:verify` **refuses to run on a fixture seeded on an earlier day** rather than loosening every age assertion to absorb the drift. 🔴 Found by the second run, not the first: `wipe()` hand-ordered its deletes and MySQL checks `accounts_invoices.parent_invoice_id` **row by row**, so one `delete where id in (…)` removed a parent before its credit note (SQLSTATE 23000); the graph has ~130 foreign keys and two self-references, so the wipe now runs with `FOREIGN_KEY_CHECKS=0` — safe only because it removes a whole tenant — while still listing every child table explicitly. Tests: `AccountsRegressionTest` (2), and the full suite is **867 passing**. |
| 381 | 🟢 **Money in — one pipeline where there were three pages** (guide §11.5 step 3) | User, 2026-09-21: "start money in". Billing (8 views), Collections (2) and bank matching were three rail destinations for one job, with **"Invoices" appearing on two of them**. `/money-in` is now the single rail item for the whole receivable side. (1) 🔴 **Stages, not tabs.** Left to right is the order a rupee actually travels: **① To bill** (handed over by pricing, not billed) → **② Drafts** (raised here, not finalized) → **③ Issued** → **④ Money in** → **⑤ Overdue**. Tabs say "here are five things"; a pipeline says "this is one thing, and here is where it is stuck", which is the question the desk opens the page with. Each stage carries its own count *and* figure, and the page opens on the first stage that has work in it. ⚠️ ① and ② are deliberately separate: one is pricing's move, the other is this desk's, and lumping them together hides whose it is. (2) 🔴 **The merge was navigation, not a rewrite.** Billing and Collections gained an `embedded` prop and are rendered *as* the stages; every list is served by the endpoint that already owned it, so no query, total or drawer changed. `MoneyInController` only **counts**. (3) ⚠️ **The fixture is what proves it.** Nine stage assertions were added to `accounts:verify` and every one is a figure already checked elsewhere by the screen that used to own it — ③ issued reads ₹7,24,800 (the four invoices and both notes, credit note signed negative), ⑤ overdue reads ₹2,06,800, the same figure the ageing, the chase queue and the Today card report. **All 90 figures still agree**, which is the evidence the merge changed nothing. (4) Today's cards now link to the **stage they counted** rather than to a page the reader has to filter. (5) The `/billing` and `/collections` routes stay for deep links and for the Boss, who reaches the ageing from Financials. New: `MoneyInController`, `MoneyIn.vue`, `awaiting` / `own_drafts` filters on the billing register, the `.fx-pipeline` styles. Tests: `MoneyInTest` (5); full suite **872 passing**. ❓ Next per §11.5: **Close the month** — five screens become one six-step checklist that knows where you are. |
| 382 | 🟢 **Close the month — five screens become one checklist that knows where you are** (guide §11.5 step 4) | User, 2026-09-21: "start close the month". Closing a month **was not an action anywhere**: it was the unposted queue, the GST register, the e-invoice list, periods and reports — five screens, none of which said whether you were done. `/close-month` is six numbered steps, each with a count and the detail behind it: **① Everything billed? → ② Everything costed? → ③ Everything posted? → ④ GST → ⑤ Close → ⑥ The statements**. The order is the information: you cannot know a month is posted before you know it is billed. (1) 🔴 **BLOCKING AND ADVISORY ARE DIFFERENT, and saying so is the point.** Only ③ stops a close, because that is the ledger's own rule — a period closed with documents outside it is a month whose reports are wrong. An unbilled shipment or a missing IRN is a **warning**: both are routinely and legitimately still open on the day a month closes, and a checklist that refused for them is one people learn to override. ⑤ names the step blocking it in words, and the confirmation lists what is still open and *not* blocking rather than hiding it. (2) 🔴 **A DRAFT IS NOT BILLED** — it carries no number, the client has never seen it and nothing has reached the ledger. Counting one as billed is how a month closes with revenue missing. (3) ④ counts only what the portal actually needs: a client with **no GSTIN is billed B2C and never goes to the IRP at all**, so it is not a gap. It also reports the tax charged in the period — the figure GSTR-1 is filed from — net of credit notes. (4) ⑥ 🔴 **`balanced` is the whole report**: a one-sided entry makes every other figure from that period untrustworthy, so it says so in those words rather than printing a total that looks fine. (5) ⚠️ **The screen shows the rule early; it does not replace it.** `closePeriod()` re-checks the unposted queue server-side and refuses independently — a test asserts both the screen's refusal and the server's. This is the step that only works because GAPS #379 made something write to that queue. (6) Ten assertions added to `accounts:verify`, against a fixture deliberately built **mid-month** — one shipment unbilled, one document unposted — because a checklist is only worth testing in the state where it says no. **All 100 figures agree.** New: `CloseMonthController`, `CloseMonth.vue`, the `.fx-steps` styles. Tests: `CloseMonthTest` (6); full suite **878 passing**. ❓ Next per §11.5: **Money out**, which needs the payment run from §11.6 to be worth its own surface. |
| 383 | 🔴→🟢 **Half the ledger did not exist: suppliers could not be paid** | User, 2026-09-21: "build the payment run then money out". A purchase voucher posted to `2100-AP` and **stayed there for ever** — there was no payment document of any kind, so the payable never came down and `1100-Bank` only ever had a receipts side. (1) 🔴 **A run raises ONE PAYMENT PER SUPPLIER, not one payment.** Each is a separate bank transfer to a separate account; a single row covering four vendors could never be reconciled against the statement. `run_ref` ties the batch together — ⚠️ **a run is an act, not an entity**, so there is no third table whose only job is to group rows. (2) ⚠️ **What is due is the GROSS**, not the net: input tax is money that actually leaves for the supplier. (3) ⚠️ **Aged from the document date, and the screen says `(assumed)`** — a voucher has no due date of its own because supplier terms are not in the schema, so the strictest reading stands in visibly rather than a generous guess nobody can audit. (4) 🔴 **No adjustment leg on a payment.** A supplier settled short is a *dispute*, not a write-off we take silently: the balance stays on the voucher and goes through the statement comparison, where somebody argues it. That is the opposite of the receipt side, where a short payment from a client genuinely can be written off — and the asymmetry is deliberate. (5) Over-paying a voucher is **refused, not clamped**. 🔒 `postLedger`: the Boss reads what is owed and cannot pay it, tested. |

(2) ⚠️ **The bare `1100-Bank` stays valid as the fallback.** Everything posted before the master existed stays exactly where it is — history is not rewritten — and the master reports that balance separately as what is still undifferentiated. (3) 🔴 **A statement can only be imported against an account of that branch**, refused with `bank_account_not_found` otherwise: importing HDFC's statement against the ICICI account reconciles money that arrived somewhere else. A bank match then raises its receipt **into the account the statement line came from**. (4) 🔐 Account number and IFSC are **encrypted at rest and never returned**; `last_four` is kept in the clear on purpose, because it is what lets somebody match a statement to an account by eye without the full number leaving the database. (5) 🔴 **Deactivate, never delete** — receipts, payments and statement lines point at it, and its ledger code carries every rupee that ever moved through it. (6) Two accounts that would derive the same code are **refused** rather than silently sharing one. 🔴 Three `$fillable` omissions caught by the fixture, all silent: `bank_account_id` was being passed to `create()` on `AccountsReceipt`, `AccountsPayment` and `BankTransaction` and **dropped on the floor** — the money posted to the fallback account and the statement line forgot where it came from. | 389 | 🔴→🟢 **"Something went wrong. Try again." — and the date bug it was hiding** | User, 2026-09-21, with a screenshot of the billing drawer showing that message twice. (1) 🔴 **The message itself was the first bug.** `messageFor()` on every finance screen read only `data.error` — but a Laravel **validation** failure answers with `message` and `errors` and **never** `error`, so every 422 on a screen that moves money fell through to "Something went wrong. Try again." A refusal nobody can read is a refusal nobody can act on. It now shows the server's own words: `error`, else the first field error, else `message`, else the status code — and "could not reach the server" is said differently from "the server refused that", because they need different actions. (2) 🔴 **The bug it was hiding is worse.** `document_date` and `due_date` were cast as `date`, which serialises as a **UTC timestamp**: a document dated 20 September in `Asia/Kolkata` reached the browser as `2026-09-19T18:30:00Z`. The drawer's header form takes the first ten characters for its date box — so it displayed **the day before**, and pressing *Save header* having touched nothing sent that day straight back. ⚠️ That silently changes **which accounting period, which ageing bucket and which month's GST** a document belongs to. Every date column on the money models is now `date:Y-m-d` — `accounts_invoices` (document and due), `accounts_receipts`, `accounts_payments`, `accounts_purchase_vouchers`, `bank_transactions`. These are dates: they have no time and no zone. A test opens a draft, reads what the drawer would read, saves it back untouched and asserts the date has not moved. Full suite **897 passing**, 117 fixture figures agree. |

| 393 | 🔴→🟢 **Closing a month was a one-way door** | User, 2026-09-22: "let's go with all this". PRD §251 has always given accounts "open/close/**reopen** `accounting_periods`" — only open and close were ever built, so closing September by mistake made every document dated inside it permanently unpostable, with no way back short of editing the database by hand. (1) 🔴 **Only the LATEST closed month of that branch may be reopened.** Reopening an older one while newer ones stay closed is how a ledger quietly goes wrong: every later month's opening figures were worked out on this one's closing arithmetic being final, and a rupee posted back into it makes them a lie without anything saying so. To reach an older month you reopen the ones after it in order, seeing each become unfinal as you go — and the refusal **names the month to unwind first**. ⚠️ A sibling branch's close does not block it: the ledger is per branch. (2) ⚠️ **It needs a reason and the reason stays on the period**, beside `closed_at` — "closed on the 3rd, reopened on the 11th because the airline's September invoice arrived late" is the sentence an auditor needs, and a status column alone cannot say it. Closing again clears the reopen: the old reason described the old close. (3) 🔒 `managePeriods` — accounts alone, not even the Boss, the same rule that guards closing one. (4) 🔴 **Found by walking it, not by a test:** `CloseMonthController` defaulted to the first OPEN period, so closing the final month left the page with nothing selected — and therefore **no reopen button**, putting the one-way door straight back where reopening was meant to remove it. It now falls back to the last month closed. Tests: `PeriodReopenTest` (7) and two more on `CloseMonthTest`; full suite **906 passing**. ❓ Next: GSTR-1 and 3B as files, then TDS. |
| 394 | 🔴→🟢 **GSTR-1 and 3B as files — and GAPS #36 closed, so the GST register can be written at all** | User, 2026-09-22: "continue with the next stage and add gst_no for agent_info as well". Two things, and the first was load-bearing for the second. **(A) `agents_info.gst_no`** — GAPS #36, open since 2026-08-27, due "before the first GST return is filed". 🔴 `gst_no` existed on `customers` and `partners` — the COUNTERPARTIES — and **nowhere on us**, so PRD §1550's rule (*"if the first two digits of the counterparty GSTIN match our branch state code, CGST + SGST, otherwise IGST"*) could never be evaluated, `GstSplitService` returned `supplier_gstin_missing`, and **not one `gst_ledger_entries` row had ever been written for any document in the system.** The register the return is filed from was structurally empty and nothing said so. ⚠️ **It hangs off the BRANCH, not the company**, as the user's wording asked and as `FreightDemoSeeder`'s own comment already assumed for partners: a GSTIN is issued per registered place of business per state, a forwarder with Mumbai and Chennai offices holds two, files two GSTR-1s, and the same client's tax splits differently from each. `gst_ledger_entries` keeps both `agent_id` and `company_id` for exactly this reason. Entered in **Settings → Finance**, validated to the 15-character shape — ⚠️ because a transposed state code files every intrastate supply as interstate and **validates cleanly at the portal**, so it is refused at entry rather than discovered by a customer who cannot claim their credit. 🔴 `Agent::$fillable` did not list it, the fourth silent `$fillable` omission on this desk; caught immediately because the fixture asserts the GSTIN it seeded. **(B) The returns.** `GstReturnService` builds GSTR-1 (**B2B · CDNR · HSN · document series**) and GSTR-3B (**3.1(a) outward · 4(A)(5) ITC · the difference**), on screen at Close-the-month ④ and as files: `?format=csv` to reconcile by eye, `?format=gstn` for the offline utility's JSON. (1) 🔴 **The return is DERIVED FROM THE DOCUMENTS, not read out of the register.** The register row is written once at finalization and only when the split was determinable — which, until today, was never. Reading the return out of it would file every month before today as empty. Both paths go through the one `GstSplitService`, so the rule is single-sourced; where a register row EXISTS and disagrees with what the documents imply now, that document is **still filed and the disagreement reported** — a counterparty GSTIN corrected after we invoiced them changes which heads the tax belongs under, and which version is the truth is not a question code can answer. (2) 🔴 **The split is applied per RATE LINE, never once on the document total.** GSTR-1 is rate-wise inside each invoice and the portal validates the tax against the rate on each; splitting the header once and apportioning it back reintroduces the rounding drift `GstSplitService` halves carefully to avoid. (3) 🔴 **THE WINDOW IS A CALENDAR MONTH — the one report here that is not period-scoped.** §6.8's strict period lockout ("a report runs over a PERIOD, never a free date range") has exactly one exception, and this is it: a return is filed monthly by law, it is the thing everything else is reconciled *against*, and **every `accounting_periods` row in this system spans a financial year**. Handing a year to the portal as one return is not a thing that can be filed. (4) 🔴 **B2CL, B2CS and EXP are deliberately NOT WRITTEN, and the documents that would go in them are named instead.** B2C needs a **place of supply** per document and nothing stores one — the counterparty has no GSTIN to take a state code from, that being the definition of unregistered, and `customers.address` is free text; the state decides which government is paid. A zero-tax supply to an unregistered party is an export, a nil-rated supply or an exempt one — three different tables — and **EXP needs a shipping bill number** we do not hold. So: `place_of_supply_unknown` and `zero_rated_needs_classification`, each carrying the document, its value and what is missing. **The exceptions are the feature** — an exception is a document the desk fixes before the deadline; a guess is a return that passes validation and is wrong, discovered by a notice months later. They are in the CSV too, at the bottom, and 3B carries their value rather than reporting a quietly smaller month. (5) 🔴 **A document whose lines do not add up to its header is refused, not filed.** The ledger posts the header tax; the return reports the lines, because only the lines carry rates. A return that disagrees with the ledger reconciles against nothing. (6) 🔴 **Found by walking a month of the fixture: the document series read "INV-0001 to INV-0004, 2 issued"** — a range spanning four numbers with a count of two, because 0002 and 0003 were dated into other months. Sequences are per fiscal YEAR and the return is per MONTH, so a month's numbers are **not** guaranteed contiguous, and the two numbers missing from that range are the exact shape of a suppressed invoice. Now one row per unbroken run, which is what `doc_issue` accepts anyway. (7) 🔴 **Found by walking it on the demo: step ④ showed DECEMBER all through September** — an empty return sitting directly under "₹6,75,934 of tax was charged", which reads as a contradiction and is really a month that has not happened yet. It defaults to the month the desk is working in, with a picker over the months that have documents; picking one re-reads the figures **from the return endpoint**, so the screen and the file are the same computation. (8) ⚠️ **3B does not decide the set-off.** Which credit offsets which head (IGST credit may be used against CGST and SGST; CGST credit may not be used against SGST) is a filing decision made in the portal against balances this system cannot see, so `difference` is labelled arithmetic, not cash payable. ⚠️ `gt`/`cur_gt` are absent from the JSON: the utility wants the PREVIOUS year's aggregate turnover, and a plausible wrong number in a field the portal validates slabs against is worse than one the accountant fills in. (9) **Demo data now contains the case that matters**: Contoso belongs to the Mumbai branch but is registered in 24 (Gujarat), so the demo has a genuine **interstate** supply — and moving it from 27 to 24 left the month's total tax **identical at ₹1,98,124.20** while relocating the entire split, which is the whole reason a wrong split hides. The carrier gained a GSTIN too, or 15 vouchers of unclaimed input credit read as a defect in 3B rather than as reference data nobody filled in. (10) The fixture's GSTIN is 27: Northstar (27) intrastate, Harbour (33) interstate, Cashflow unregistered and zero-rated. **47 new hand-computed assertions** in `accounts:verify` — `360,000` taxable, `27,900` CGST, `27,900` SGST, `9,000` IGST, `64,800` tax, ITC `25,200` a head, difference `2,700 / 2,700 / 9,000`, and the export's `300,000` reported as unfilable — plus a cross-check that **close-the-month ④'s tax (summed off document HEADERS) equals the return's tax (summed off LINE ITEMS, split into heads, credit note netted)**: two tables, two computations, one answer. **All 164 figures agree.** New: `GstReturnService`, `GstReturnController`, `GstReturnTest` (23), migration `2026_09_22_140000_add_gstin_to_agents_info`, `FinanceSettingsController::saveGstin`. ❓ **Still open, and now visible rather than silent:** nothing back-writes `gst_ledger_entries` for documents finalized before today, and **purchase vouchers never wrote one at all** though PRD §1555 says they should — 3B's ITC is computed from the vouchers directly, so it is right, but the register is an outward-only record. ❓ Next: **TDS**, the last item on §11.6. |
| 395 | 🔴→🟢 **TDS, both directions — the last item on §11.6** | User, 2026-09-22: continue with TDS; this session (2026-09-25) finished and shipped it. The live defect it started from: a client who deducts TDS pays short, and all three existing receipt resolutions were wrong — `short_paid` ages a client who paid correctly, `write_off` books it as a bank charge, `discount` books it as revenue given up. It is none of those: **tax already paid on our behalf, recoverable against our own income-tax liability — an asset.** Added a fourth resolution, `tds`. (1) 🔴 **THE TWO DIRECTIONS ARE NEVER NETTED.** Outward — we withhold from vendors (a trucker under 194C) — is a **liability**, `2300-TDS-Payable`, due by the **7th of next month** (30 April for March, computed rather than described — the exception everybody misses). Inward — clients withhold from us — is an **asset**, `1400-TDS-Receivable`, claimed via Form 26AS, no deadline. Netting them by accident would report a company holding nothing when it is holding both. (2) 🔴 **The base excludes GST** — tax is deducted on the service value, never on the tax charged on it; proportional for part payments. (3) 🔴 **The rate is stamped on the entry, never re-derived** — rates change with every Finance Act, and re-deriving historical deductions from today's rate table would silently restate returns already filed and challans already paid. (4) 🔴 **Crossing the ANNUAL threshold makes the whole year deductible**, with catch-up net of what was already withheld; if the catch-up exceeds the payment, the payment is withheld in full and `still_owed` is reported. (5) No PAN → 20% (s.206AA); a s.197 certificate wins over everything, including the no-PAN rate. (6) Seeded rates (194C/194C-IND/194J/194H/194I/194I-B) are **editable defaults to be checked against the current Finance Act, not law** — the Settings → Finance screen says so on screen, not just in a comment. (7) Second defect fixed on the way: `ReceiptController::adjustment()` ignored each allocation's own resolution and sent *everything* to `4900-Sales-Adjustments`, so a write-off posted differently depending on whether the receipt screen or bank matching closed it — `linesForReceipt()` now takes one leg per resolution. (8) TDS is Close-the-month's **advisory step ⑤** (Close → ⑥, Statements → ⑦) — never blocking, only step ③ is. **UI, all new this session:** the register (Financials → TDS register: both sides, one quarter, Form 26Q CSV, the ledger-vs-register cross-check, unclassified vendors named), the rate editor (Settings → Finance, seeded lazily per branch the first time the screen is read so the desk never sees an empty table), and `tds_section`/s.197 override editable inline on each partner in the directory (`manageFinanceSettings` — accounts and the Boss, the same desk that sets the rate table, not `triage`). 🔴 **Found by walking the demo, not by a test:** `BillingDemoSeeder`'s reset deleted `accounts_payments` and rebuilt `accounts_ledger_entries` on every reseed but never touched `tds_entries` — a stale deduction from a prior run stayed in the register forever, pointed at a payment id that no longer existed, and the TDS screen's own ledger-cross-check correctly flagged it as "a posting went somewhere else" when the actual fault was the seeder's own hygiene. Fixed by deleting `tds_entries` by `agent_id`, branch-scoped like the ledger it feeds, alongside everything else the reseed already clears. 🔴 **Also found while wiring the register's own link:** `Financials.vue` never read `$route.query.view` — every `?view=X` link from Close-the-month, Money out and elsewhere landed on the default "Waiting to be billed" regardless of what it said, a pre-existing gap now fixed for every view, not only this one's. New: `TdsService`, `TdsController`, `tds_rates`/`tds_entries` migration, `partners.tds_section`/`tds_rate_override`, `FinanceSettingsController::saveTdsRate`, `PartnerController::updateTds`. Tests: `TdsTest` (22); full suite **956 passing**, `npx jest` **106 passing**. ❓ Still open, flagged not silently fixed: `gst_ledger_entries` is written on post, not finalize, as PRD §1555 says it should be; purchase vouchers never write one at all; nothing back-writes the register for documents finalized before 2026-09-22; the GSTN JSON has not been round-tripped through the real offline utility. §11.6 is now clear. |
| 396 | 🔴→🟢 **Checked TDS and GSTR-1/3B end to end (user, 2026-09-25/26) — three UI gaps and one seeder gap, all behind backend that was already correct** | User: "check the demo end to end", then "check the GSTR-1/3B screens too". Every one of these was the same shape: the backend had it right, the screen never asked it the right question, and nothing automated caught it because `accounts:verify` and the feature test suites check figures, not whether a control is reachable or a table has rows. **(1) The vendor TDS section was free text with a datalist** — a typo silently meant `section_not_configured` (reported, not fatal, but easy to miss).
