Root cause: `InvoiceController::post()` writes the register row via `writeGstRegister()`, a sibling call to the ledger write, not part of it — and both `AccountsRegressionSeeder` and `BillingDemoSeeder` post invoices through `LedgerPostingService::write()` directly (the seeder's own stated philosophy: "through the same service the controllers use" — true of the ledger, not of the register). `accounts:verify` has no assertion on this table at all, so 176/176 passed throughout. `writeGstRegister()` made `public`, called from both seeders right after they post an invoice. `GstReturnService` derives GSTR-1/3B from the documents directly, never from this table, so **no return figure moved** — confirmed identical before and after, in the CSV, the portal JSON, and Close-the-month step ④. Only the register itself, and the ledger-vs-register cross-check both it and the TDS register depend on, went from silently empty to real. Verified in the browser, not just via API: a live payment run, a manufactured short payment settled as `tds`, and the GST register populated after reseeding — all end to end, not just asserted. Tests unchanged throughout: full suite **956 passing**, `npx jest` **106 passing**, `accounts:verify` **176 figures agree** (same 176, same figures — nothing here was a return-figure bug). ❓ Still open from #394, untouched by this pass: nothing back-writes `gst_ledger_entries` for documents finalized before 2026-09-22; purchase vouchers never write one; the GSTN JSON has not been round-tripped through the real offline utility. |
| 397 | 🔴→🟢 **The three GST-register decisions, made and built** | User, 2026-09-26, answering the three items flagged since #394. **(1) Written on POST, not finalize — kept as is.** PRD §1555 says finalize; the code writes on post, and the decision was that the code is right: post is the one irreversible commit, the same moment the ledger and the TDS register are written, and a finalized document can still be voided before it ever posts. No code change; every place that claimed "written when a document is finalized" (the `GstReturnService` and `RegisterController` docblocks, the register screen's subtitle and footnote, `GstReturnTest`'s ❓) now says post and cites this. **(2) Purchase vouchers now write the input-credit side of the register.** `PurchaseVoucherController::post()` calls a new `writeGstRegister()`, the buy-side mirror of the invoice one. 🔴 The roles are reversed — the vendor is the supplier and we are the counterparty — but `GstSplitService::split()` only compares two state codes, so OUR GSTIN stays in its "supplier" slot on both sides to keep `supplier_gstin_missing` meaning *ours is absent* everywhere. The row write itself moved into `LedgerPostingService::writeGstRegisterRow()`, shared by both controllers — the class's own docblock already said invoices and vouchers written in two controllers is "how the sell side ends up with a GST split the buy side does not have". 3B was already right (its ITC is computed from the vouchers directly); only the register was one-sided. **(3) Backfill — `php artisan accounts:backfill-gst-register [--dry-run]`.** Writes the row for every POSTED taxable invoice and voucher that lacks one, through the same `writeGstRegister()` posting calls. Idempotent; zero-tax documents are not candidates (they never get a row by design, and counting them as "skipped" told somebody to fix a GSTIN that was not the problem); a still-undeterminable split is counted and named. `created_at` on a backfilled row is when it was written — nothing reads the register by date. On the reseeded dev DB it finds nothing, because the seeders now write rows; the pre-fix path is proven by `GstReturnTest`. **Three things found while building it:** 🔴 **(a) the register total mixed tax charged with input credit** the moment purchases landed in it — a liability and an asset summed into one figure nobody holds. Now two totals, output and input, and a direction per row on screen ("Charged" / "Paid (input credit)"). 🔴 **(b) Credit notes were ADDED to the register total**, breaking the standing rule that a credit note subtracts in every total — on the demo, both `CN-…` rows' ₹432 IGST counted as tax charged. Pre-existing; fixed because this was the total being rewritten. Rows are now signed, so the column adds up to the total under it. 🔴 **(c) My own #396 fix caused duplicate register rows.** `BillingDemoSeeder`'s reset never cleared `gst_ledger_entries` — it never needed to while nothing wrote one — so each reseed doubled every invoice's row (16 → 32 → 48 before it was caught). Cleared by branch now, and `FreightDemoSeeder` clears it too, plus `tds_entries`. 🔴 **(d) `FreightDemoSeeder` could not re-run once `BillingDemoSeeder` had raised supplier payments** (fixed on request, same day): its purge predated the whole billing layer — it deleted every demo voucher's lines, then was refused on `accounts_payment_allocations` → `accounts_purchase_vouchers`, leaving vouchers with no lines until the billing seeder ran again. The same shape as #391, fixed the same way: foreign-key checks OFF for the purge, and every table the billing layer writes now listed (payments and their allocations, receipts and theirs, chases, the bank master, supplier statements and lines, rate cards, TDS rates, brokerage and consol details, the GST and TDS registers). ⚠️ **With checks off, `ON DELETE CASCADE` and `SET NULL` do not fire either** — five tables were cleaned by a cascade from `jobs` and three relied on `SET NULL`; the purge now does each of those by hand, so switching checks off changes nothing about what it removes or keeps. Verified by running it over live billing data (the exact case that failed), twice, then the billing seeder twice: stable counts, ledger balanced to the paisa, no voucher without lines, no register row pointing at a missing document, no duplicate. (The first full run also stopped in `DemoMailAttachmentsSeeder` on an unhealthy local ClamAV container, up 5 days; restarted, healthy, the rebuild completed.) The billing demo now reports **36** purchase vouchers where it said 60 — 60 had built up over the week the base demo could not be rebuilt; 36 is the clean-slate figure and holds across reruns. 🔴 **(e) `AccountsRegressionSeeder`'s reset leaked every run** (fixed on request, same day): it deletes its branches and vouchers with checks off but never listed payments, their allocations, `tds_entries` or `tds_rates` — so each reseed left one more of each on a branch that no longer exists (16 payments and 16 allocations, 13 TDS entries, 54 TDS rates on the dev DB, found by counting rows on dead branches across every table with an `agent_id`; those four were the only ones). Now listed. Proved by two reseeds with the dead-branch counts unchanged, and all 176 figures agreeing; the rows already leaked — unreachable by the wipe, since their company is gone — were cleared once in a transaction, leaving no allocation pointing at a missing voucher anywhere. Tests: `GstReturnTest` +4 (voucher writes the input side, unregistered vendor writes nothing, two totals with the credit note subtracted, backfill writes what posting missed and nothing twice); `AccountsWorkQueueTest` updated for the two-sided totals. `accounts:verify` **176 figures agree**. |
| 398 | 🔴→🟢 **"Cost to book" as a queue — the last row of §11.6** | User, 2026-09-26: "build the cost to book queue". Money out ① was a count linking to Profitability: you could see which billed shipments had no cost, and book it nowhere. 🔴 **The real blocker was the lock, not the missing screen.** The cost sheet locks the moment the invoice leaves draft, and that lock covered the BUY side too — and a billed shipment with no cost is billed by definition, so there was no path at all. **Decisions (asked, not assumed):** (1) after billing, the buy side opens for **adding a cost only** — no changing or removing a line, and **nothing is ever added to a posted voucher** (refused with `voucher_posted`, before billing too: the sheet would happily append to a voucher posted the day before, changing a document whose journal was already written); the sell side stays locked, corrections by credit note. (2) Booked **inline on Money out ①**. (3) **Accounts only** — a new `bookLateCost` gate, rather than borrowing `postLedger`; pricing's sheet stays locked once billed, as before. **Built:** `GET /money-out/to-cost` — the same definition as the ① count so the list and the number cannot disagree, billed amount net of tax, the default supplier as a NAME (the cost sheet creates the airline partner the first time it is owed money; a list that did that would write a partner every time Money out opened, so the default is sent as no vendor and the sheet resolves it itself); booking via the existing `POST /jobs/{job}/cost-sheet/lines` with `side=buy`, audited as `costsheet.late_cost_booked`. GST % is required, never defaulted — a guessed rate books input credit nobody was charged. The demo now leaves each branch's most recently billed shipment uncosted (its airline invoice "not in yet") so the queue has something real in it; Close-the-month ② reports those two as well, which is the true reading. | 402 | 🔴→🟢 **General billing — an invoice with no shipment behind it, credit and debit notes included** | User, 2026-09-26, after sharing Logi-Sys's Billing → General screen: "go ahead with your recommendation but you'll give the option to raise credit note and keep all the calculations in mind". Answers the question open since the status list: `accounts_invoices.job_id` was NOT NULL, so billing that is not for a shipment (warehousing, a service sold on its own) had nowhere to go. **Not their menu tree:** one Billing screen, a **General** tab beside Invoices and Debit notes, and a **Not for a shipment** switch when raising an invoice — which names the branch billing it instead of a job. Notes raise against it as against any invoice (the drawer's *Raise a note*) and carry its NULL job. **Schema:** `job_id` NULLABLE — not a placeholder "general job", which every report counting shipments would have counted — plus `chk_invoice_job_for_shipment_documents`: only an invoice and its notes may lack a job; the database refuses a brokerage or consol bill without one. **Posting:** Dr 1200-AR / Cr **4100-Other-Operating-Revenue** (new) / Cr GST — kept out of 4000-Freight-Revenue so freight stays freight; its credit note keeps the mirror (Dr 4900 / Dr GST / Cr AR); its debit note posts to 4100 like its parent. Same INV series, number minted on finalize like any invoice. **Every calculation, proved by `GeneralBillingTest` with figures worked by hand** — the journals; the GST register (credit note subtracted); GSTR-1 (B2B for the invoice, CDNR for its notes) and 3B; the ageing and the credit gate (credit note subtracted); inward TDS on it (paid in full, an asset); the cost-to-book queue and profitability. 🔴 **Profitability reports it BESIDE the shipments, never among them** — no cost side, so mixed in it would lift every margin — as a `general` block on the same filters (branch, client, document date; nothing on a mode or lane filter), shown on How-we're-doing as "plus ₹X billed not for a shipment". `accounts:verify`'s cross-checks now state the true identity: **P&L revenue = shipment revenue + general**, and so the net. 🔴 **Three calculation faults found on the way, fixed:** (1) Money out ①'s count skipped a NULL job but its rupee SUM did not — general billing would have shown as cost to book that the list beside it could not account for; (2) the invoice list's margin preview, looking up cost on a NULL job, would have shown a general invoice's whole sale as margin — now NULL; (3) 🔴 **the `NOT IN` trap:** `jobs.id NOT IN (SELECT job_id FROM accounts_invoices)` is UNKNOWN for every row once one job_id is NULL, so it silently returns nothing — the demo's cost-sheet queue went from 6 to 0 the moment a general invoice existed. The whole app was swept: every live query uses NULL-safe `NOT EXISTS`; the only two `NOT IN`s were in `BillingDemoSeeder`, both fixed. The demo now raises one general invoice and a credit note per branch (bonded warehousing, SAC 996729) and is otherwise unchanged — 2 brokerage, 2 consol, 6 cost sheets waiting, stable across rebuilds. ⚠️ **Scope:** a general invoice bills a CLIENT for now — partner-billed general billing needs its own receivable and a check of receipts and the AR cross-check. ⚠️ **Flagged, not fixed — both pre-existing, both breaking the standing rules:** (a) the sales dashboard's revenue per rep sums GRAND totals (tax included), counts VOID invoices (`status != draft`) and leaves credit notes out instead of subtracting them; (b) the invoice list's margin preview (Financials → Waiting to be billed) is sell `net_amount` minus buy `net_amount` — both tax-inclusive, so not net of tax. The regression fixture holds no general invoice yet; the proof is the feature test. |
| 403 | 🔴→🟢 **Three figures that broke the standing money rules — the sales revenue, the margin preview, the credit gate's currency** | User, 2026-09-26, on the two flagged in #402 and the one found while fixing them. **(1) Sales revenue.** The rep's revenue on the sales dashboard summed GRAND totals (18% of tax in it), of invoices alone with **VOIDS INCLUDED** (`status != draft`), and left credit notes out — so it never fell when a client was given money back. The per-client snapshot behind branch target progress and the client book (`sales:compute-snapshots`) had the same revenue fault and more: it ADDED every credit note, to revenue and to what is owed (so the ageing buckets and credit utilisation rose when money was given back), and summed foreign-currency bills at face value. Now both: revenue is the SUBTOTAL, in INR at each document's rate, credit notes subtract and debit notes add, drafts and voids never; what is owed is signed and in INR too. General billing to a rep's client counts toward their revenue. **(2) Margin preview** (Financials → Waiting to be billed): sell `net_amount` minus buy `net_amount` — both tax-INCLUSIVE, so a sale at 18% on a zero-rated cost read 18% of the sale better than it was; and the sell side was in the document's currency while the cost is rupees, so a dollar invoice's margin subtracted rupees from dollars. Now line AMOUNTS on both sides, the sell side in INR, and the columns relabelled *Sell (net of tax)* / *Cost (net of tax)* so they add up on screen. **(3) The credit gate** counted a USD invoice's grand total at face value against a rupee limit — USD 10,000 used ₹10,000 of it — so a client billed in dollars could run up many times their limit; now in INR at each document's rate, as the ageing already did. 🔴 **Found fixing it:** `finalize()` ran the gate on CREDIT NOTES as if they added their total, so a client already over their limit could be **refused the credit note giving them money back**. A credit note is now never gated. `RevenueRulesTest` (6) — each test verified to FAIL on the old code and pass on the new, so none is decorative. The regression fixture is all INR, so its 176 figures are unchanged; the currency cases live in the test. ⚠️ **Flagged, not fixed:** the sales snapshots are per client per MODE, so general billing (no mode) is in the rep's revenue on the dashboard but not in the per-mode snapshots behind branch target progress. |
| 404 | 🔴→🟢 **General billing counts toward the branch revenue target** | User, 2026-09-26: "make general billing count toward branch revenue targets".
